  try {
    if (window.__mintlifyInitialSidebarScrollDone) return;
    window.__mintlifyInitialSidebarScrollDone = true;

    var path = (window.location.pathname || '/').split('#')[0].split('?')[0];
    if (path.endsWith('/index')) path = path.slice(0, -6);
    else if (path === 'index') path = '';

    var candidates = [];
    if (path) candidates.push(path);
    if (path.startsWith('/')) candidates.push(path.slice(1));
    else candidates.push('/' + path);

    var item = null;
    for (var i = 0; i < candidates.length && !item; i++) {
      var matches = document.querySelectorAll('[id="' + candidates[i].replace(/"/g, '\\"') + '"]');
      for (var j = 0; j < matches.length; j++) {
        if (matches[j].closest('#sidebar, #sidebar-content')) {
          item = matches[j];
          break;
        }
      }
    }
    if (!item) return;

    var parent = item.closest('[data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport"]');
    if (!parent) {
      parent = item.parentElement;
      while (parent) {
        var style = getComputedStyle(parent);
        if (style.overflowY === 'auto' || style.overflowY === 'scroll') break;
        parent = parent.parentElement;
      }
    }
    if (!parent) return;

    var parentRect = parent.getBoundingClientRect();
    var itemRect = item.getBoundingClientRect();
    if (itemRect.top >= parentRect.top && itemRect.bottom <= parentRect.bottom) return;

    var itemTopRelative = itemRect.top - parentRect.top + parent.scrollTop;
    parent.scrollTop = itemTopRelative - parentRect.height / 2 + itemRect.height / 2;
  } catch (e) {}
})();</script></div></div></div></div></div></nav><main id="content-container" class="lg:flex-1 lg:min-w-0"><script>document.documentElement.setAttribute('data-page-mode', "none");</script><span class="fixed inset-0 bg-background-light dark:bg-background-dark -z-10 pointer-events-none" id="background-color"></span><style data-custom-css-index="0" data-custom-css-path="style.css">#footer div:last-child {

stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="min-w-0 break-words cursor-pointer">Overview</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">GLM-4.6V series are Z.ai’s iterations in a multimodal large language model. GLM-4.6V scales its context window to 128k tokens in training, and achieves SoTA performance in visual understanding among models of similar parameter scales. Crucially, GLM-4.6V integrate native Function Calling capabilities for the first time. This effectively bridges the gap between “visual perception” and “executable action,” providing a unified technical foundation for multimodal agents in real-world business scenarios.</span>

7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" class="w-full overflow-hidden pl-8 pr-px"><h3 class="mt-2 cursor-pointer" contentEditable="false" data-component-part="step-title">Native Multimodal Tool Use</h3><div data-component-part="step-content" class="prose dark:prose-invert"><span data-as="p">Traditional tool usage in LLMs often relies on pure text, requiring multiple intermediate conversions when dealing with images, videos, or complex documents—a process that introduces information loss and engineering complexity.</span><span data-as="p">GLM-4.6V is equipped with native multimodal tool calling capability:</span><ul>
<li><strong>Multimodal Input:</strong>  Images, screenshots, and document pages can be passed directly as tool parameters without being converted to text descriptions first, minimizing signal loss.</li>
<li><strong>Multimodal Output:</strong>  The model can visually comprehend results returned by tools—such as searching results, statistical charts, rendered web screenshots, or retrieved product images—and incorporate them into subsequent reasoning chains.</li>
</ul><span data-as="p">This native support allows GLM-4.6V to close the loop from perception to understanding to execution, enabling complex tasks like mixed text-image content creation and visual web searching.</span></div></div></div><div id="capabilities-&amp;-scenarios" role="listitem" class="step group/step step-container relative flex items-start pb-5"><div data-component-part="step-line" class="absolute w-px h-[calc(100%-2.75rem)] top-11 bg-gray-200/70 dark:bg-white/10" contentEditable="false"></div><div class="absolute ml-[-13px] py-2" data-component-part="step-number" contentEditable="false"><div data-component-part="step-indicator" class="group/step-indicator relative size-7 shrink-0 rounded-full bg-gray-50 dark:bg-white/10 text-xs text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-50 font-semibold flex items-center justify-center"><div class="[[data-component-part=step-indicator]:has(a:focus-visible)_&amp;]:opacity-0">2</div><div class="absolute" data-component-part="step-number-anchor-link"><a href="#capabilities-&amp;-scenarios" class="flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 focus-visible:opacity-100" aria-label="Navigate to header"><div id="panel-intelligent-image-text-content-creation-%26-layout-0" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="intelligent-image-text-content-creation-%26-layout" tabindex="0" class="prose dark:prose-invert overflow-x-auto [&amp;_[data-table-wrapper]]:![--page-padding:0px] [&amp;_[role=&quot;listitem&quot;]]:pl-4 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)_img]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child[data-table-wrapper]]:pt-0!" data-component-part="tab-content"><span data-as="p">GLM-4.6V can accept multimodal inputs—mixed text/image papers, reports, or slides—and automatically generate high-quality, structured image-text interleaved content.</span><ul>
<li><strong>Complex Document Understanding:</strong>  Accurately understands structured information from documents containing text, charts, figures, and formulas.</li>
<li><strong>Visual Tool Retrieval:</strong>  During generation, the model can autonomously call search tools to find candidate images or crop key visuals from the source multimodal context.</li>
<li><strong>Visual Audit &amp; Layout:</strong>  The model performs a “visual audit” on candidate images to assess relevance and quality, filtering out noise to produce structured articles ready for social media or knowledge bases.</li>
</ul><video src="https://cdn.bigmodel.cn/static/4.6v/en/Case-图文-英文-1208.m4v" controls=""></video></div><div id="panel-visual-web-search-%26-rich-media-report-generation-1" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="visual-web-search-%26-rich-media-report-generation" tabindex="0" class="prose dark:prose-invert overflow-x-auto [&amp;_[data-table-wrapper]]:![--page-padding:0px] [&amp;_[role=&quot;listitem&quot;]]:pl-4 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)_img]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child[data-table-wrapper]]:pt-0! hidden" data-component-part="tab-content"><span data-as="p">GLM-4.6V delivers an end-to-end multimodal search-and-analysis workflow, enabling the model to move seamlessly from visual perception to online retrieval and finally to structured and illustrated report generation. Across the entire process, it maintains multimodal context awareness and performs reasoning grounded in both textual and visual information.</span><ul>
<li><strong>Intent Recognition and Search Planning:</strong>  For queries containing images, text, or both, GLM-4.6V identifies the user’s search intent and determines what information is needed. It then autonomously selects and triggers the appropriate search tools (e.g., text-to-image search, image-to-text search, or image-to-image matching) to retrieve relevant data.</li>
<li><strong>Multimodal Results Alignment:</strong>  The model reviews the mixed visual and textual information returned by the search tools, identifies the parts most relevant to the query, and uses them to support the subsequent reasoning and generation process.</li>
<li><strong>Reasoning &amp; Rich Media Report Generation:</strong>  Leveraging relevant visual and textual cues retrieved from search, the model performs the necessary reasoning steps and generates a structured report, seamlessly integrating both visual and textual elements to match the task requirements.</li>
</ul></div><div id="panel-frontend-replication-%26-visual-interaction-2" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="frontend-replication-%26-visual-interaction" tabindex="0" class="prose dark:prose-invert overflow-x-auto [&amp;_[data-table-wrapper]]:![--page-padding:0px] [&amp;_[role=&quot;listitem&quot;]]:pl-4 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)_img]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child[data-table-wrapper]]:pt-0! hidden" data-component-part="tab-content"><span data-as="p">We have optimized GLM-4.6V for frontend development, significantly shortening the “design to code” cycle.</span><ul>
<li><strong>Pixel-Level Replication:</strong>  By uploading a screenshot or design file, the model identifies layouts, components, and color schemes to generate high-fidelity HTML/CSS/JS code.</li>
<li><strong>Visual Debugging:</strong>  Users can circle an area on a generated page screenshot and give natural language instructions (e.g., “Move this button left and make it dark blue”). The model automatically locates and corrects the corresponding code snippet.</li>
</ul><video src="https://cdn.bigmodel.cn/static/4.6v/en/Case-复刻网站-英文-1208.m4v" controls=""></video></div><div id="panel-long-context-understanding-3" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="long-context-understanding" tabindex="0" class="prose dark:prose-invert overflow-x-auto [&amp;_[data-table-wrapper]]:![--page-padding:0px] [&amp;_[role=&quot;listitem&quot;]]:pl-4 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child:not(p)_img]:mt-0 [&amp;&gt;:first-child[data-table-wrapper]]:pt-0! hidden" data-component-part="tab-content"><span data-as="p">GLM-4.6V aligns its visual encoder with a 128K context length, giving the model a massive memory capacity. In practice, this equates to processing ~150 pages of complex documents, 200 slide pages, or a one-hour video in a single inference pass.</span><ul>
<li><strong>Financial Analysis:</strong>  In testing, GLM-4.6V successfully processed financial reports from four different public companies simultaneously, extracting core metrics across documents and synthesizing a comparative analysis table without losing key details.</li>
</ul><video src="https://cdn.bigmodel.cn/static/4.6v/en/Case-财报-英文-1208.m4v" controls=""></video><ul>
<li><strong>Video Understanding:</strong>  The model can perform global summarization on long videos while retaining the ability to perform fine-grained reasoning on temporal clues, such as summarizing goal events and timestamps in a full football match.</li>

style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="min-w-full! h-full children:my-0! children:shadow-none! children:bg-transparent!"><div class="font-mono whitespace-pre leading-6"><pre class="shiki shiki-themes github-light-default dark-plus" style="background-color:#ffffff;--shiki-dark-bg:#0B0C0E;color:#1f2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4" language="text"><code language="text" numberOfLines="57"><span class="line"><span>Of course. Here is a research report that provides a comprehensive, illustration-integrated analysis of the two benchmarks described in the literature.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Research Report: A Comparative Analysis of AIGCBench and FETV for Evaluating AI-Generated Video</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>This report analyzes two key research papers that introduce novel benchmarks for evaluating AI-generated video content. The first, AIGCBench, focuses on a comprehensive evaluation of Image-to-Video (I2V) generation, while the second, FETV, proposes a benchmark for the fine-grained evaluation of open-domain Text-to-Video (T2V) generation. By examining their methodologies, datasets, evaluation metrics, and results, we can understand the current state and challenges of automated video generation assessment.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>1. AIGCBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Image-to-Video Generation</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>AIGCBench is designed to address the limitations of existing benchmarks by providing a unified and scalable framework for evaluating I2V models. Its primary goal is to offer a fair comparison of different algorithms under equivalent conditions.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Framework and Datasets:</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The AIGCBench framework is divided into three core modules: datasets, video generation models, and evaluation metrics. As illustrated in Figure 1, the benchmark utilizes two types of datasets: real-world video-text pairs and a novel set of image-text pairs generated through a proprietary pipeline.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>!&lt;image_ref&gt;[1, [[102, 75, 887, 296]], &quot;Figure 1: Illustration of the AIGCBench framework.&quot;]&lt;/image_ref&gt;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>This pipeline, detailed in Figure 2, involves combining subjects, behaviors, backgrounds, and styles to create diverse prompts, which are then processed by GPT-4 for enhancement before being fed to a text-to-image model (Stable Diffusion) to generate the final image.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>!&lt;image_ref&gt;[1, [[102, 71, 908, 466]], &quot;Figure 2: Image-text dataset generation pipeline and results.&quot;]&lt;/image_ref&gt;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Evaluation Metrics and Results:</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>AIGCBench evaluates I2V models across four critical dimensions:</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Control-Video Alignment: How well the generated video matches the input image.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Motion Effects: The quality and plausibility of the motion.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Temporal Consistency: The coherence of the video across time.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Video Quality: The overall aesthetic and technical quality.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The benchmark evaluates five state-of-the-art models: VideoCrafter, I2VGen-XL, SVD, Pika, and Gen-2. The quantitative results, summarized in Table 2, show that Gen-2 performs best in video quality, while SVD and Pika show strong performance in control-video alignment and temporal consistency.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>!&lt;table_ref&gt;[1, [[90, 75, 907, 235]], &quot;Table 2: Quantitative analysis for different Image-to-Video algorithms.&quot;]&lt;/table_ref&gt;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Qualitative comparisons in Figure 3 visually demonstrate the performance differences. For example, in the &quot;knight running&quot; prompt, Gen-2 produces a highly detailed and aesthetically pleasing video, whereas VideoCrafter and I2VGen-XL struggle to preserve the spatial structure of the original image.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>!&lt;image_ref&gt;[1, [[92, 71, 910, 722]], &quot;Figure 3: Qualitative comparison of five I2V algorithms on three different prompts.&quot;]&lt;/image_ref&gt;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>A user study with 42 participants was conducted to validate the metrics. The results, visualized in a radar chart in Figure 4, show that Pika excels in motion effects and temporal consistency, while Gen-2 is favored for video quality.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>!&lt;image_ref&gt;[1, [[385, 75, 614, 266]], &quot;Figure 4: Radar chart showing user preferences for different I2V algorithms.&quot;]&lt;/image_ref&gt;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>2. FETV: A Benchmark for Fine-Grained Text-to-Video Evaluation</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>FETV addresses the need for more nuanced evaluation of T2V models by introducing a multi-aspect categorization of prompts. Unlike benchmarks that provide only a single overall score, FETV breaks down evaluation into specific categories to reveal a model&#x27;s strengths and weaknesses.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Multi-Aspect Categorization:</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>FETV&#x27;s core innovation is its categorization of text prompts based on three orthogonal aspects, as shown in Figure 1:</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Major Content: The primary subject (e.g., people, animals, scenery).</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Attribute Control: Specific attributes the prompt aims to control (e.g., color, speed, motion direction).</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Prompt Complexity: The complexity of the text prompt (simple, medium, complex).</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>This framework allows for a &quot;fine-grained evaluation&quot; that goes beyond a single score.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>!&lt;image_ref&gt;[2, [[181, 71, 815, 314]], &quot;Figure 1: Illustration of FETV&#x27;s multi-aspect categorization.&quot;]&lt;/image_ref&gt;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Evaluation and Results:</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>FETV evaluates four T2V models: CogVideo, Text2Video-zero, ModelScopeT2V, and ZeroScope. The evaluation is performed manually across four perspectives: static quality, temporal quality, overall alignment, and fine-grained alignment.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The results, presented in radar charts in Figure 5 and Figure 6, reveal significant variations in model performance across different categories. For instance, Figure 5 shows that ModelScopeT2V performs well on &quot;static quality&quot; for &quot;people&quot; and &quot;scenery&quot; but poorly on &quot;artifacts&quot; and &quot;vehicles.&quot;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>!&lt;image_ref&gt;[2, [[267, 71, 731, 472]], &quot;Figure 5: Manual evaluation of static and temporal video quality across different major contents.&quot;]&lt;/image_ref&gt;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Furthermore, Figure 6 demonstrates that models struggle with controlling specific attributes. For example, in the &quot;fine-grained alignment&quot; chart, all models show weakness in controlling &quot;event order.&quot;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>!&lt;image_ref&gt;[2, [[181, 525, 807, 722]], &quot;Figure 6: Manual evaluation of video-text alignment across different attributes and complexities.&quot;]&lt;/image_ref&gt;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The benchmark also includes leaderboards (Figure 7) that rank the models based on their overall performance in static quality, temporal quality, and alignment.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>!&lt;image_ref&gt;[2, [[175, 71, 817, 196]], &quot;Figure 7: Leaderboard on the FETV benchmark based on manual evaluation.&quot;]&lt;/image_ref&gt;</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>3. Comparative Analysis and Conclusion</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Both AIGCBench and FETV represent significant steps forward in the standardized evaluation of AI-generated video, but they target different tasks and evaluation philosophies.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Scope: AIGCBench focuses specifically on the I2V task, evaluating how well a model can animate a static image. FETV, in contrast, is designed for the more open-ended T2V task, where the model must generate a video from a text description.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Evaluation Granularity: FETV&#x27;s primary strength is its &quot;fine-grained&quot; approach, using a detailed categorization system to pinpoint model weaknesses. AIGCBench provides a more holistic, four-dimensional score, offering a broader performance overview.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Methodology: AIGCBench combines both reference-dependent (video-dependent) and reference-free (video-free) metrics, along with human validation. FETV relies heavily on comprehensive manual evaluation to establish a ground truth, which it then uses to diagnose the reliability of automatic metrics.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>In conclusion, these two benchmarks provide a more robust and multi-faceted way to assess AI video generation models. AIGCBench offers a comprehensive framework for fair I2V model comparison, while FETV pushes for a deeper, more nuanced understanding of T2V model capabilities. Together, they highlight the importance of diverse and detailed evaluation in driving progress in the field of AIGC.</span></span>

<span class="line"><span>!&lt;image_ref&gt;[2, [[181, 71, 815, 314]], &quot;Figure 1: Illustration of FETV&#x27;s multi-aspect categorization.&quot;]&lt;/image_ref&gt;</span></span>

language="text" numberOfLines="23"><span class="line"><span>I now need to address the user’s question, and first, I have to recall the breeds of the cats in the image. Let’s start with the first cat on the left: it has stripes and a raised tail, so it should be a tabby cat (e.g., an American Shorthair or British Shorthair, but this type of striped pattern is typically tabby). The second cat is also striped with a slightly curved tail—could it also be a tabby? Wait, no. The third cat is light brown and white; could it be a Siamese cat? Because Siamese cats are usually pointed or bicolor with a light coat. The fourth cat is a small gray-and-white kitten—could it be a Ragdoll? Do Ragdolls have long fur? No, the kitten in the image appears to have short fur, so maybe an American Shorthair? Wait, let me think again: the fifth cat is striped with a very high, raised tail—could it be a Maine Coon? Because Maine Coons have long, fluffy tails, yes, the fifth cat has a large, bushy tail, so it should be a Maine Coon. The sixth cat is a small gray shorthair—probably a British Shorthair? The seventh cat is a long-haired gray cat with a fluffy tail—could it be a Norwegian Forest Cat? Because Norwegian Forest Cats have long fur and large tails. The eighth cat is a small striped kitten—likely an American Shorthair tabby.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Now let’s count the number of cats in the image: counting left to right, there are 8 cats total. Let me confirm each one:</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>First cat on the left: tabby pattern, short fur, upright tail—likely a &quot;tabby cat&quot;.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Second cat: tabby pattern, curved tail, short fur—likely a &quot;tabby cat&quot; (same breed, different individual).</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Third cat: light brown and white with pointed ears—likely a &quot;Siamese cat&quot; (Siamese cats are characterized by pointed coloration, pointed ears, and large eyes).</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Fourth cat: gray-and-white, small build, short fur—likely an &quot;American Shorthair&quot; (American Shorthair: short fur, round face).</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Fifth cat: striped with an especially long, fluffy tail—likely a &quot;Maine Coon&quot; (Maine Coons have long, dense fur on their tails).</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Sixth cat: small gray shorthair—likely a &quot;British Shorthair&quot; (British Shorthair: round face, short fur).</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Seventh cat: long-haired gray with a fluffy tail—likely a &quot;Norwegian Forest Cat&quot; (Norwegian Forest Cats have long fur, large tails, and a sturdy build).</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Eighth cat (last on the right): small striped kitten, short fur—likely an &quot;American Shorthair tabby&quot;.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Next, I need to verify if the breed names are accurate and if there are more precise labels. For example, the third cat: Siamese cats are marked by pointed coloration (e.g., an M-shaped marking on the head), pointed ears, and blue or green eyes. The third cat in the image has pointed ears and a light brown-and-white coat, which matches Siamese characteristics. The fifth cat’s tail is indeed long and dense, so it is a Maine Coon. The seventh cat fits the profile of a Norwegian Forest Cat with its long fur and large tail. The others are common tabby cats, and American/British Shorthairs are common shorthair breeds.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Then, I need to define the position of each cat using bbox_2d, which represents coordinates in the format [x1, y1, x2, y2] (where y1 = top, y2 = bottom, x1 = left, x2 = right). For example:</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The first cat (far left): approximate bbox is [100, 150, 190, 820]</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The second cat: [180, 330, 310, 850]</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The third cat: [300, 350, 430, 900]</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The fourth cat: [420, 520, 510, 920]</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The fifth cat: [500, 260, 605, 850]</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The sixth cat: [600, 450, 700, 870]</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The seventh cat: [680, 120, 800, 830]</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>The eighth cat: [800, 480, 885, 830]</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>These coordinates are approximate (based on position in the image) and do not require precise pixel values—only general ranges.</span></span>
<span class="line"><span>Finally, organize this into a JSON list where each element is a dictionary. The label should be the breed plus a serial number (e.g., &quot;Tabby Cat-1&quot;, &quot;Tabby Cat-2&quot;, and so on).</span></span>

<span class="line"><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#C586C0">                    if</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> (</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">data</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">getChoices</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">() </span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">!=</span><span style="color:#0550AE;--shiki-dark:#569CD6"> null</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> &amp;&amp;</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> !</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">data</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">getChoices</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">().</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">isEmpty</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">()) {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#4EC9B0">                        Delta</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE"> delta</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> =</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE"> data</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">getChoices</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">().</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">get</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">(</span><span style="color:#0550AE;--shiki-dark:#B5CEA8">0</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">).</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">getDelta</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">();</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">                        System</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">out</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">print</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">(delta </span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">+</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178"> &quot;</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D7BA7D">\n</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">);</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">                    }},</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#6E7781;--shiki-dark:#6A9955">                // Process streaming response error</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">                error </span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#569CD6">-&gt;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE"> System</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">err</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">println</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">(</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&quot;</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D7BA7D">\n</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">Stream error: &quot;</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> +</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE"> error</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">getMessage</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">()),</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#6E7781;--shiki-dark:#6A9955">                // Process streaming response completion event</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">                () </span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#569CD6">-&gt;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE"> System</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">out</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">println</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">(</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&quot;</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D7BA7D">\n</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">Streaming response completed&quot;</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">)</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">            );</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">        } </span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#C586C0">else</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> {</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">            System</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE">err</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">println</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">(</span><span style="color:#0A3069;--shiki-dark:#CE9178">&quot;Error: &quot;</span><span style="color:#CF222E;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4"> +</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#9CDCFE"> response</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">.</span><span style="color:#8250DF;--shiki-dark:#DCDCAA">getMsg</span><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">());</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">        }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">    }</span></span>
<span class="line"><span style="color:#1F2328;--shiki-dark:#D4D4D4">}</span></span>
</code></pre></div></div></div></div><div class="code-block-fade-overlay print:hidden" data-fade-overlay="true" aria-hidden="true" image.`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`Motion Effects: The quality and plausibility of the motion.`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`Temporal Consistency: The coherence of the video across FETV: A Benchmark for Fine-Grained Text-to-Video Evaluation`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`FETV addresses the need for more nuanced evaluation of T2V models by introducing a multi-aspect categorization of prompts. Unlike benchmarks that provide only a single overall score, FETV breaks down evaluation into specific categories to reveal a model's strengths and weaknesses.`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`Multi-Aspect Categorization:`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`FETV's core innovation is its categorization of text prompts based on three orthogonal aspects, as shown in Figure 1:`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`Major Content: The primary subject (e.g., people, animals, scenery).`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`Attribute Control: Specific attributes the prompt aims to control (e.g., color, speed, motion direction).`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`Prompt Complexity: The complexity of the text prompt (simple, medium, complex).`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`This framework allows for a \"fine-grained evaluation\" that goes beyond a single score.`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`!\u003cimage_ref\u003e[2, [[181, 71, 815, 314]], \"Figure 1: Illustration of FETV's multi-aspect categorization.\"]\u003c/image_ref\u003e`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`Evaluation and Results:`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`FETV evaluates four T2V models: CogVideo, Text2Video-zero, ModelScopeT2V, and ZeroScope. The evaluation is performed manually across four perspectives: static quality, temporal quality, overall alignment, and fine-grained alignment.`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`The results, presented in radar charts in Figure 5 and Figure 6, reveal significant variations in model performance across different categories. FETV, in contrast, is designed for the more open-ended T2V task, where the model must generate a video from a text description.`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`Evaluation Granularity: FETV's primary strength is its \"fine-grained\" approach, using a detailed categorization system to pinpoint model weaknesses. AIGCBench provides a more holistic, four-dimensional score, offering a broader performance overview.`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`Methodology: AIGCBench combines both reference-dependent (video-dependent) and reference-free (video-free) metrics, along with human validation. FETV relies heavily on comprehensive manual evaluation to establish a ground truth, which it then uses to diagnose the reliability of automatic metrics.`})}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.span,{className:`line`,children:_jsx(_components.span,{children:`In conclusion, these two benchmarks provide a more robust and multi-faceted way to assess AI video generation models.
